In January 2013, *[GQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GQ)* placed Beyoncé on its cover, featuring her atop its "100 Sexiest Women of the 21st Century" list.[^cite_note-GQstory-468] [VH1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VH1) listed her at number one on its 2013 list of the "100 Sexiest Artists".[^cite_note-469] Beyoncé is known for rarely granting interviews, especially to traditional media; she largely stopped participating in conventional press interviews in 2013.[^cite_note-z936-470] Active on social media, Beyoncé has over 300 million followers on Instagram; she was the [most-followed account on the platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most-followed_Instagram_accounts) until 2015 and remains one of its most-followed users.[^cite_note-u800-471][^cite_note-j071-472]

Beyoncé's lighter skin tone and styling choices have drawn criticism, with some arguing that they may perpetuate [Eurocentric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocentric) beauty standards and contribute to issues of [colorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorism), particularly regarding the underrepresentation of darker-skinned Black women in mainstream media.[^cite_note-Jones-473][^cite_note-r187-474][^cite_note-z429-475] Emmett Price, a professor of music at [Northeastern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_University), wrote in 2007 that he thinks race plays a role in many of these criticisms, saying white celebrities who dress similarly attract fewer comments.[^cite_note-Jones-473] In 2008, [L'Oreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Oreal) denied accusations of whitening her skin in their [Feria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feria) hair color advertisements;[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477] In 2013, Beyoncé criticized [H\&M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H\&M) for their proposed "retouching" of promotional images of her, requesting only "natural pictures be used".[^cite_note-478] In 2007, she became the second African-American woman to appear on the cover of the *[Sports Illustrated Swimsuit Issue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Illustrated_Swimsuit_Issue)* magazine,[^cite_note-People2-479] and *People* recognized her as the best-dressed celebrity.[^cite_note-480]

## Legacy

Main article: [Cultural impact of Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Beyonc%C3%A9)

See also: [Destiny's Child § Legacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destiny's_Child#Legacy)

Beyoncé's silhouette is shown leaning back and singing into a microphone, surrounded by smoke

Beyoncé performing during her *I Am...* Tour in 2009

Beyoncé is widely recognized as one of the most influential figures in music history.[^cite_note-481] *Billboard* named her the [greatest pop star of the 21st century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard's_Greatest_Pop_Stars_of_the_21st_Century) in 2024.[^cite_note-\:5-482] Beyoncé has repeatedly been named as the defining artist of both the 2000s and 2010s.[^cite_note-483] Writing in *The New Yorker*, Rosen called her "the most important and compelling popular musician of the twenty-first century \[...] the result, the logical end point of a century-plus of pop".[^cite_note-w377-484] She is often deemed one of the greatest entertainers of all time,[^cite_note-Greatestandinfluential-485] and critics have hailed several of her [albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9_albums_discography), [singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9_singles_discography), [music videos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9_videography), and [live performances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Beyonc%C3%A9_live_performances) as amongst the greatest of all time.[^cite_note-486]

Beyoncé is hailed as one of the most culturally significant figures of the 21st century.[^cite_note-487] Her enduring popularity as a musician has received extensive recognition and is often praised for its longevity.[^cite_note-\:5-482][^cite_note-488] She is credited with revolutionizing the [music industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry), transforming the [distribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry#Business_structure), [promotion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Promoter_\(entertainment\)), and consumption of music.[^cite_note-\:5-482][^cite_note-489][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETrier-Bieniek2016190-490] Her 2013 album *Beyoncé* popularized [surprise releases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surprise_release), prompting widespread adoption of unconventional promotional strategies throughout the 2010s and 2020s.[^cite_note-Five-491][^cite_note-\:5-482][^cite_note-t380-492] Beyoncé is also recognized for reviving the music video as an art form, popularizing the visual album format, and largely inspiring the International Federation of the Phonographic Industry to move Global Release Day from Tuesday to Friday.[^cite_note-493]

Beyoncé has been credited with revitalizing the [album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Album) format in an era dominated by [singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_\(music\)), with albums becoming increasingly cohesive and narrative-led.[^cite_note-494] Her use of staccato rap-singing and chopped and re-pitched vocals helped them become prominent in 21st-century music.[^cite_note-495] Beyoncé's work transcends traditional [genre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_genre) boundaries, creating new artistic standards that shaped [contemporary music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_music) and helped music artists to move between and beyond genre confines.[^cite_note-y025-186][^cite_note-496][^cite_note-o973-497][^cite_note-498] She has also helped expand the popularity of many subgenres, introducing different audiences to R\&B,[^cite_note-e913-499][^cite_note-k630-500] country,[^cite_note-x130-501][^cite_note-e729-502] dance,[^cite_note-q857-503] and house,[^cite_note-q376-504] and [Afrobeats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrobeats).[^cite_note-i783-505][^cite_note-506] Beyoncé is known for transforming concert tours into cultural and artistic events.[^cite_note-e547-507]

Beyoncé has been recognized for setting new standards for success in the modern era,[^cite_note-y025-186][^cite_note-o973-497] with musicians from across genres, generations, and countries citing her as a [major influence on their career](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Beyonc%C3%A9#Influence_on_other_artists). [Taylor Swift](5422144.md) called her a major influence, crediting her with showing other artists how to oppose industry standards and create new opportunities.[^cite_note-v223-508][^cite_note-j113-509] Lady Gaga said Beyoncé inspired her to become a musician,[^cite_note-510] while [Rihanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rihanna) was motivated to pursue music after watching Beyoncé.[^cite_note-r617-511] [Ariana Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_Grande) said she learned to sing by mimicking artists like Beyoncé,[^cite_note-f796-512] while [Adele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adele) described her as part of her artistic influence since she was a preteen.[^cite_note-y428-513] McCartney and [Garth Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garth_Brooks) have also cited Beyoncé's live performances as inspirational, with the latter recommending that both new and veteran musicians analyze these performances to improve their work.[^cite_note-514][^cite_note-j408-515]

Beyoncé's influence extends into academia and cultural institutions, with her artistry and career being [extensively studied in universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Beyonc%C3%A9#Academic_study) around the world.[^cite_note-a239-516][^cite_note-k636-517] Museum exhibitions analyze her impact on music, fashion, activism, and popular culture.[^cite_note-a666-518][^cite_note-x362-519] She has popularized phrases that entered mainstream culture: "put a ring on it" (from "Single Ladies") to signify a marriage proposal;[^cite_note-y873-520] "I woke up like this" (from "[Flawless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flawless_\(Beyonc%C3%A9_song\))"), which sparked a trend of morning [selfies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selfie);[^cite_note-l592-521] and "boy, bye" (from "Sorry"), used as a way to break up with a romantic partner.[^cite_note-w868-522] In January 2012, Australian research scientist Bryan Lessard named a species of [horse-fly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horse-fly), *[Scaptia beyonceae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scaptia_beyonceae)*, after her, due to the insect's distinctive golden hairs on its [abdomen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdomen).[^cite_note-523] Several [wax figures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wax_figure) of Beyoncé are found at [Madame Tussauds Wax Museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussaud's_Wax_Museum) in major cities around the world.[^cite_note-524]

## Achievements

See also: [List of awards and nominations received by Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Beyonc%C3%A9) and [List of awards and nominations received by Destiny's Child](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Destiny's_Child)

Beyoncé smiling in front of a wall, in a red dress, holding an award

Beyoncé with her [Video of the Year](476903.md) award at the [2009 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_MTV_Video_Music_Awards)

Beyoncé has [received numerous awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Beyonc%C3%A9) and is one of the most-awarded performers of all time.[^cite_note-525] She is one of the [best-selling music artists of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best-selling_music_artists_of_all_time), having sold over 200 million records worldwide—and an additional 60 million with Destiny's Child.[^cite_note-526][^cite_note-f396-527] With sixty-four certifications, Beyoncé was the most-certified artist of the 2000s by the Recording Industry Association of America;[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529] she became the most-certified female artist of all time in 2024.[^cite_note-530] Several of her tours are amongst the highest-grossing by women, and she is one of the [highest-grossing live acts in history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_live_music_artists#All-time_ranking).[^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532]

Beyoncé has thirty-five [Grammy Awards](44635.md)—both as a solo artist and as a member of a group—making her the [most-awarded act at the award show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_records#Most_Grammys_won).[^cite_note-533][^cite_note-x696-534] She also has ninety-nine nods, making her the most-nominated artist.[^cite_note-g408-93][^cite_note-q050-535] As the recipient of thirty [MTV Video Music Awards](295217.md) (VMAs),[^cite_note-VMA_DC-536] she is tied with Taylor Swift for the most in its history.[^cite_note-p230-537][^cite_note-c321-538] At the [BET Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET_Awards), Beyoncé is the most awarded and nominated individual, with thirty-two awards and seventy-five nominations.[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-540] She also leads at the [Soul Train Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_Train_Music_Awards) and the [NAACP Image Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NAACP_Image_Awards), with twenty-five and thirty-two wins, respectively.[^cite_note-541][^cite_note-542][^cite_note-543] At the [2011 *Billboard* Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Billboard_Music_Awards), she was honored with the [*Billboard* Millennium Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Millennium_Award).[^cite_note-i799-544][^cite_note-n028-545] She has [set numerous world records over her career](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Beyonc%C3%A9#World_records).[^cite_note-h837-546]

For her role in *Dreamgirls*, Beyoncé was nominated for [Best Original Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Original_Song) for "Listen" and [Best Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Actress_%E2%80%93_Motion_Picture_Musical_or_Comedy) at the [Golden Globe Awards](142724.md).[^cite_note-547] The film *Lemonade* (2016) won a [Peabody Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peabody_Award) in 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvery2017775–777-548] In 2022, "Be Alive" was nominated for the Academy Award and Golden Globe Award for Best Original Song.[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-d444-264] In June 2021, Beyoncé was honored as the Top Touring Artist of the 2010s at the [*Pollstar* Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollstar_Awards).[^cite_note-550] In August 2025, she won a [Primetime Emmy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award_for_Outstanding_Costumes_for_a_Variety,_Nonfiction,_or_Reality_Programming) for costume design for her [2024 NFL Halftime Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9_2024_NFL_Halftime_Show).[^cite_note-b519-551] *Billboard* ranked her at number three on its 2025 "Top 100 Women Artists of the 21st Century" list and at seventeen as part of Destiny's Child.[^cite_note-552] *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* named Beyoncé one of the [100 most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) in 2013,[^cite_note-w144-553] 2014,[^cite_note-i114-554] and 2023.[^cite_note-t616-555] She occupied the sixth place for the magazine's 2016 [Person of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Person_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-556]

## Wealth

Beyoncé is one of the [wealthiest musical artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_music_artists_by_net_worth); as of March 2026, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* estimates her net worth to be \$1 billion.[^cite_note-n203-557] The magazine named Beyoncé the annual [highest-earning female musician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_highest-earning_musicians#Female) in 2008,[^cite_note-Forb-558] 2010,[^cite_note-559] 2014,[^cite_note-560] and 2017.[^cite_note-561] *Forbes* began reporting on her earnings in 2008, calculating that the \$80 million earned between June 2007 and June 2008 made her the second-highest-paid musician in 2008.[^cite_note-562][^cite_note-Forb-558] On the [*Forbes* Celebrity 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_Celebrity_100) list, Beyoncé was placed fourth in 2008 and 2009,[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] second in 2010,[^cite_note-565] and fourth in 2013.[^cite_note-566] She ranked at number one on the Celebrity 100 list in 2014, earning an estimated \$115 million.[^cite_note-567] *Billboard* named Beyoncé the highest-paid musician of 2016, with estimated earnings of \$62.1 million.[^cite_note-568] She was listed as the third [highest-paid musician of the 2010s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_highest-earning_musicians#2010s) by *Forbes*, earning \$685 million.[^cite_note-569]

*Forbes* placed Beyoncé and Jay-Z at number one on the "World's Highest-Paid Celebrity Couples" list, collectively earning \$78 million and \$107.5 million in 2012 and 2016, respectively.[^cite_note-2016-forbes-570][^cite_note-u620-571] The couple made it into 2011's *[Guinness Book of World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_Book_of_World_Records)* as the "highest-earning power couple" for collectively earning \$122 million in 2009.[^cite_note-572] They officially became a billion-dollar couple in 2017, when *Forbes* estimated a combined net worth of \$1.16 billion.[^cite_note-573]

## Other ventures

### Social activism

In 2012, Beyoncé held a [fundraiser](339302.md) for Obama's [presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_2012_presidential_campaign)[^cite_note-574] and voted for him in the [presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election).[^cite_note-u255-575] Three years later, Beyoncé attended a celebrity fundraiser for [2016 presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election) nominee [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton)[^cite_note-576] and headlined in a concert for Clinton the weekend before the election.[^cite_note-577] She endorsed the bid of [Beto O'Rourke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beto_O'Rourke) during the [2018 US Senate election in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_US_Senate_election_in_Texas).[^cite_note-578] Beyoncé endorsed [Joe Biden](145422.md) for president during the [2020 election](48410011.md) and [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) in [2024](59568546.md),[^cite_note-g557-579][^cite_note-580] giving the latter permission to use "Freedom" as the official song for [her presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign).[^cite_note-581]

Beyoncé identifies as a "modern-day [feminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminist)".[^cite_note-582] Her self-identification incited debate about whether her feminism is aligned with older, more established feminist ideals; [Annie Lennox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Lennox) referred to her use of the word feminist as "feminist lite".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeidhase2015128–131-583][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2017110-584] Beyoncé publicly aligned with feminism by sampling [Chimamanda Ngozi Adichie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chimamanda_Ngozi_Adichie)'s 2013 [TEDx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEDx) speech "We should all be feminists" in "Flawless", released later that year.[^cite_note-585] Adichie described Beyoncé's feminism as leaning toward [heteropatriarchal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heteropatriarchal) ideals, emphasizing men's needs and diverging from her own views.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWard2017159-586] Beyoncé performed at the 2014 MTV Video Music Awards before a giant "Feminist" backdrop.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2017110-584] Some critics suggest that her portrayal of empowerment tends to be individualized, with limited engagement in structural issues affecting marginalized groups.[^cite_note-t098-587][^cite_note-r680-588]

In December 2012, Beyoncé joined a coalition of celebrities in the "Demand a Plan" campaign—an initiative led by US mayors to urge federal action on gun control legislation following the [Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting).[^cite_note-i342-589] In 2013, she endorsed [same-sex marriage](92656.md) via Instagram[^cite_note-z756-590] and voiced opposition to North Carolina's [Public Facilities Privacy & Security Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Facilities_Privacy_&_Security_Act), a law criticized for discriminating against the [LGBT community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_community).[^cite_note-r654-591] In February 2017, she supported [transgender youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_youth) following president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s federal rollback of protections for transgender students in public schools.[^cite_note-a762-592] During her 2023 Renaissance World Tour, she incorporated queer activism through [flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_flag), [voguing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voguing), and many other forms of LGBT expression.[^cite_note-k333-593]

Beyoncé has spoken against [police brutality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_brutality_in_the_United_States) toward African-Americans.[^cite_note-n891-594][^cite_note-g006-595] She attended a 2013 rally after the acquittal of [George Zimmerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Zimmerman) in [the killing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Trayvon_Martin) of [Trayvon Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trayvon_Martin),[^cite_note-w611-596] and featured the mothers of Martin, [Michael Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Michael_Brown), and [Eric Garner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Garner) holding photos of their deceased sons in the music video for "Freedom".[^cite_note-i061-597] Her 2016 single "Formation" was interpreted as a critique of law enforcement, though she clarified it was a celebration of her heritage.[^cite_note-598] Performing the song during the 2016 Super Bowl halftime show, with dancers dressed in outfits referencing the Black Panther Party, sparked backlash from [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) figures and law enforcement groups.[^cite_note-599] Beyoncé responded to the criticism by selling "Boycott Beyoncé" merchandise on her tour.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace2017195-600]

### Philanthropy

In 1999, Beyoncé, her mother Tina Knowles, and former Destiny's Child bandmate Kelly Rowland established the Knowles-Rowland Center for Youth.[^cite_note-d243-601] Following [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina) in 2005, Beyoncé, Solange, Tina, and Rowland co-founded the Survivor Foundation to offer transitional housing for displaced families and support the construction of new homes.[^cite_note-q514-602] They extended aid after [Hurricane Ike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Ike) in 2008, with Beyoncé donating \$100,000 to the Gulf Coast Ike Relief Fund.[^cite_note-z713-603] In 2007, she launched the Knowles-Temenos Place Apartments, a housing development that accommodates 43 displaced individuals.[^cite_note-o313-604][^cite_note-n353-605]

Beyoncé sitting while smiling in front of a dark blue curtain

Beyoncé at the opening of the Cosmetology Center in 2010

After learning about [Phoenix House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_House)—a nonprofit focused on drug and alcohol rehabilitation—during the filming of *Cadillac Records* in 2008, Beyoncé donated her entire \$4 million salary from the film to the organization.[^cite_note-q870-606][^cite_note-o313-604] She later founded the Beyoncé Cosmetology Center, a program at Phoenix House that provides a cosmetology training course.[^cite_note-o313-604][^cite_note-e596-607] In 2010, she donated approximately \$2 million to Haiti earthquake relief efforts.[^cite_note-608] In April 2011, Beyoncé participated in the National Association of Broadcasters Education Foundation's campaign against [child obesity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_obesity) by reworking "Get Me Bodied".[^cite_note-609][^cite_note-610] She was an ambassador for the 2012 [World Humanitarian Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Humanitarian_Day) campaign,[^cite_note-q091-611] and took part in [Gucci](844136.md)'s 2013 "Chime for Change" initiative to promote global female empowerment, to which she donated \$500,000[^cite_note-t908-612][^cite_note-613] She also founded [BeyGOOD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BeyGood) during the [Mrs. Carter Show World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mrs._Carter_Show_World_Tour). The organization supports education, entrepreneurship and disaster relief programs.[^cite_note-614] By 2014, Beyoncé and her family had provided \$7 million in cumulative support to her childhood church, St. John’s Downtown, and its related nonprofit efforts in Houston.[^cite_note-615]

Beyoncé and Jay-Z donated tens of thousands of dollars to post bail for Black Lives Matter (BLM) protesters in [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore) and [Ferguson, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferguson,_Missouri), and contributed to building the infrastructure needed to establish BLM chapters across the US.[^cite_note-616] In April 2016, she pledged to fund ten college scholarships for students in financial need.[^cite_note-617] In June, she donated over \$82,000 to the [United Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Way) of [Genesee County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesee_County,_Michigan) to assist victims of the [Flint water crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flint_water_crisis) and provided financial support for fourteen Michigan students pursuing higher education.[^cite_note-b490-618] That August, Beyoncé and Jay-Z donated \$1.5 million to civil rights organizations including BLM, [Hands Up United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hands_Up_United), and the Trayvon Martin Foundation.[^cite_note-p393-619]

In August 2017, during [Hurricane Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Harvey), Beyoncé launched BeyGood Houston to support those affected and donated \$75,000 worth of new mattresses to survivors.[^cite_note-w119-620] Later that month, she released a remix of [J Balvin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J_Balvin) and [Willy William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willy_William)'s "[Mi Gente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mi_Gente)", pledging all proceeds to disaster relief efforts across Puerto Rico, Mexico, the US, and the Caribbean in response to Hurricanes Harvey, [Irma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Irma), and [Maria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Maria), as well as the [Chiapas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Chiapas_earthquake) and [Puebla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Puebla_earthquake) earthquakes.[^cite_note-621] In April 2020, Beyoncé donated \$6 million to the National Alliance on Mental Illness, [UCLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCLA), and local community-based organizations to provide mental health and wellness services for essential workers during the COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-x242-622] She featured on the remix of [Megan Thee Stallion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megan_Thee_Stallion)'s "Savage" (2020), with proceeds supporting Bread of Life Houston's COVID-19 relief efforts.[^cite_note-h781-623]

Beyoncé worked with the [Feminist Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminist_Coalition) in the [End SARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_SARS) movement in [Nigeria](21383.md) to cover medical costs for injured protestors, legal fees for arrested protestors, and resources for those in need. Beyoncé also raised public awareness about the [Anglophone Crisis](57170868.md) in [Cameroon](5447.md), [ShutItAllDown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ShutItAllDown) in [Namibia](21292.md), [Zimbabwean Lives Matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zimbabwean_Lives_Matter) in [Zimbabwe](34399.md), and the Rape National Emergency in [Liberia](17791.md).[^cite_note-624] In December 2020, Beyoncé donated \$500,000 to alleviate the housing crisis in the US caused by the cessation of the [eviction moratorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_eviction_moratoriums_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-b896-625] In partnership with BeyGood, in 2024 she introduced an annual grant program to support cosmetology students and professional hair stylists with financial assistance.[^cite_note-s656-626] In January 2025, she donated \$2.5 million to Los Angeles fire relief funds amidst the [wildfires that month in Southern California](78831062.md).[^cite_note-r918-627] In 2026, Beyoncé donated \$1 million for earthquake relief in Colombia, supporting food and housing needs in the affected [Chocó region](549667.md).[^cite_note-628] On her 45th birthday in September, she made a \$2 million gift to the [Studio Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_Museum_in_Harlem) in Harlem.[^cite_note-629]

### Entrepreneurship

In 2005, Beyoncé and Tina launched [House of Deréon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Der%C3%A9on), a women's fashion line inspired by three generations of women in their family.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2011123-630] The two also established Beyond Productions to handle licensing and brand management for House of Deréon and its junior line, Deréon.[^cite_note-BusWire-631] The collection features sportswear, denim offerings with fur, outerwear, and accessories like handbags and footwear.[^cite_note-BusWire-631] In 2005, Beyoncé partnered with House of Brands, a footwear company, to produce a range of shoes for House of Deréon.[^cite_note-632]

In January 2008, [Starwave Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starwave) launched Beyoncé Fashion Diva, a [mobile game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_game) with a social networking component, featuring the House of Deréon collection.[^cite_note-BusWire-631] That same year, she founded Parkwood Entertainment, an entertainment company established as an imprint of Columbia Records.[^cite_note-v234-633][^cite_note-g083-634][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarvey2017118-635] It has since grown to include a music division, signing artists such as [Chloe x Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chloe_x_Halle).[^cite_note-o260-636] In July 2009, Beyoncé and her mother launched a junior apparel label, Sasha Fierce for Deréon, for the back-to-school season. The collection included sportswear, outerwear, handbags, footwear, eyewear, lingerie, and jewelry.[^cite_note-637]

In April 2016, Beyoncé launched [Ivy Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_Park), a 50–50 joint venture with fashion retailer [Topshop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topshop). The brand's name was inspired by her daughter, Blue Ivy, Beyoncé's favorite number, four (IV), and the park where she used to run in Texas.[^cite_note-z132-638] Following allegations that Topshop owner [Philip Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Green) had sexually harassed, bullied, and racially abused employees, Beyoncé bought out his 50 percent stake in the company.[^cite_note-i920-639][^cite_note-x374-640] In April 2019, she partnered with [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) as a creative collaborator to relaunch Ivy Park and develop new apparel and footwear, with the first collection debuting in January 2020.[^cite_note-641][^cite_note-j068-642] The brand struggled financially, and by March 2023, Beyoncé and Adidas mutually ended their partnership.[^cite_note-k115-643] That same month, Beyoncé collaborated with [Balmain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balmain_\(fashion_house\)) creative director [Olivier Rousteing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Rousteing) on a couture collection inspired by *Renaissance*. With sixteen looks—one for each track—it was the first time a Black woman led the design of a Parisian couture house collection.[^cite_note-o240-644] In February 2024, Beyoncé launched [Cécred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9cred), a hair care line designed to serve a wide range of hair textures.[^cite_note-o420-645]

#### Endorsements and partnerships

Beyoncé began her partnership with [Pepsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepsi) in 2002.[^cite_note-646] Two years later, she starred in a [gladiator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladiator)-themed commercial for the company alongside [Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears), [Pink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_\(singer\)), and [Enrique Iglesias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Iglesias).[^cite_note-647] Beyoncé signed a \$50 million endorsement deal with Pepsi in 2012.[^cite_note-648] Beyoncé has also collaborated with [Tommy Hilfiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Hilfiger) for the fragrances [True Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Star_\(perfume\))—for which she recorded a cover of "[Wishing on a Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wishing_on_a_Star)"—and True Star Gold.[^cite_note-649][^cite_note-650] In 2007, she promoted [Emporio Armani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emporio_Armani)'s Diamonds fragrance.[^cite_note-651] Beyoncé launched her own fragrance line with [Heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_\(fragrance\)) in 2010, followed by [Heat Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_Rush) in 2011 and Pulse later in the same year.[^cite_note-652][^cite_note-653][^cite_note-654] Beyoncé also has had deals with [American Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Express),[^cite_note-Forb-558] [Nintendo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo),[^cite_note-655] and [L'Oréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Or%C3%A9al).[^cite_note-656]

In March 2015, Beyoncé became a co-owner of the music streaming platform Tidal, alongside several other artists.[^cite_note-657] In November 2020, she entered a multi-year partnership with fitness and media company [Peloton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peloton_Interactive).[^cite_note-658] In 2021, Beyoncé and Jay-Z partnered with [Tiffany & Co.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiffany_&_Co.) for the company's "About Love" campaign. Beyoncé became the fourth woman, and first Black woman, to wear the 128.54-carat [Tiffany Yellow Diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiffany_Yellow_Diamond).[^cite_note-659] The campaign drew criticism, as the diamond is considered a [blood diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_diamond) and a symbol of British colonial exploitation in Africa.[^cite_note-662][^cite_note-663][^cite_note-664]

In August 2024, Beyoncé announced [SirDavis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SirDavis), a [whiskey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whiskey) in collaboration with [Moët Hennessy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mo%C3%ABt_Hennessy) developed for years prior and co-founded with [master distiller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_distiller) Dr. Bill Lumsden.[^cite_note-665] Beyoncé acquired [LVMH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LVMH)'s stake in SirDavis in August 2026, becoming the sole owner of the brand.[^cite_note-u891-666] In October 2024, [Levi's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi's) launched a four-part global campaign titled "Reiimagine" with Beyoncé, spotlighting women's history with the company and featuring her *Cowboy Carter* track "[Levii's Jeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levii's_Jeans)".[^cite_note-667]

In May 2026, Beyoncé co-chaired the [Met Gala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Met_Gala) alongside [Nicole Kidman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicole_Kidman) and [Venus Williams](167378.md), which [*Vogue*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\)) announced in December 2025. The event, themed "[Costume Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costume_Art)", marked her first Met Gala appearance since 2016.[^cite_note-w395-668][^cite_note-669]
